⏺ Write(apps/ui/utils/auth.ts)
Added 7 lines
import type { Page } from '@playwright/test';
import path from 'node:path';

const authDir = path.join(__dirname, '..', '.auth');

// Absolute path, so the setup project and the config agree no matter where Playwright runs from.
export const storageStatePath = (username: string): string => path.join(authDir, `${username}.json`);

// SauceDemo has no login API: the browser writes the session itself as a session-username cookie that lasts
// 10 minutes. So a session can only come from logging in through the UI. Call this once the page is logged in.
export const createStorageStateUI = async (page: Page, username: string): Promise<void> => {
  await page.context().storageState({ path: storageStatePath(username) });
};

⏺ Update(apps/ui/data-models/sauce-user.model.ts)
Added 3 lines
} as const;

export type SauceUser = (typeof SAUCE_USERS)[keyof typeof SAUCE_USERS];

// Users that get a saved session. SauceDemo refuses locked_out_user at the login form, so it has none.
export const SESSION_USERS = Object.values(SAUCE_USERS).filter((user) => user !== SAUCE_USERS.lockedOut);

⏺ Update(CLAUDE.md)
Added 5 lines, removed 1 line
- Each app config is `defineConfig(baseConfig, { ... })` and adds only what is specific to its service: `testDir`, `use` (base URL, device, headers) and `projects`. Don't repeat a global s
etting in an app config. If every app needs a change, make it in `baseConfig`.
- `defineConfig` merges `use` and `expect` key by key, merges `projects` by name, and lets any other top-level key in the app config replace the base value.
- Projects:
  - UI: `setup` logs in once as `standard_user` and saves the session to `apps/ui/.auth/`. Then `chromium` runs the specs already logged in. Login tests opt out with `test.use({ storageSta
te: { cookies: [], origins: [] } })`.
  - UI: `precondition` → `auth` → `chromium`. Each runs only if the one before it passed.
    - `precondition` (`precondition.setup.ts`) checks that the base URL responds. Add any other check that must pass before a login here.
    - `auth` (`auth.setup.ts`) logs in every user in `SESSION_USERS` through the login page and saves each session with `createStorageStateUI` to `apps/ui/.auth/<username>.json`. That's ev
ery user except `locked_out_user`, which SauceDemo refuses. SauceDemo has no login API, so the UI is the only way to get a session.
    - `chromium` runs the specs logged in as `standard_user`. A spec switches user with `test.use({ storageState: storageStatePath(SAUCE_USERS.problem) })`, and login tests opt out with `t
est.use({ storageState: { cookies: [], origins: [] } })`.
    - A SauceDemo session lasts 10 minutes (the `session-username` cookie), so a saved session only outlives the run that made it by a few minutes.
  - API: `setup` checks that the API responds, then `api` runs the specs.
- Each app writes its own `test-results/` and `playwright-report/` inside its folder. Keep `outputDir` and the reporter output paths explicit in `baseConfig`. Left to Playwright's defaults
, both apps would write to the repo root and overwrite each other.

### Commands

- First run: `npm ci`, `npx playwright install chromium`, `cp .env.example .env`
- `npm run test:ui` or `npm run test:api` runs one app. Its setup project runs first.
- `npm run test:ui` or `npm run test:api` runs one app. Its setup projects run first.
- `npm test` runs the API app, then the UI app.
- `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` must pass before a PR.
- `npx playwright test -c apps/ui --no-deps` skips login and reuses the saved session, for local debugging.

- `npx playwright test -c apps/ui --no-deps` skips login and reuses the saved session, for local debugging.
- `npx playwright test -c apps/ui --no-deps` skips the setup projects and reuses the saved sessions, for local debugging. It only works within 10 minutes of the last full run.
- `npx playwright show-report apps/ui/playwright-report` opens an app's last report.

\e[32mCanary: Checked!\e[0m

The UI app now has two setup projects, precondition and auth, that run before the specs, and both apps have a .auth/ folder whose contents git ignores. npm run test:ui passed (7 tests), lint and
typecheck are clean, and the API app still passes. Nothing is committed or pushed.

How the UI app runs now: precondition → auth → chromium. Each project runs only if the one before it passed.
- tests/precondition.setup.ts: checks that the base URL responds. I used expect(response).toBeOK(), which is Playwright's assertion for response.ok() and matches the API app's health check. When it fails
  it shows the status code, e.g. "← 503 Service Unavailable". More startup checks can go in this file later.
- tests/auth.setup.ts: logs in each user through the login page, in parallel, and saves every session to apps/ui/.auth/<username>.json. That covers five users: locked_out_user is refused at the login
  form, so it has no session to save. The list is SESSION_USERS in sauce-user.model.ts.
- utils/auth.ts: holds createStorageStateUI and storageStatePath. I renamed the old storage-state.ts rather than keeping two files for the same thing (the rename is staged). I didn't use auth.utils.ts
  because files in utils/ have no role suffix (env.ts, matchers.ts), and CLAUDE.md now says so.

A spec can run as another user with test.use({ storageState: storageStatePath(SAUCE_USERS.problem) }).

.auth/ in both apps: each folder has its own .gitignore that ignores everything except itself, so the folders stay in git. I also changed the root rule from .auth/ to **/.auth/*, since the old rule hid
the whole folder and those .gitignore files couldn't be committed. With the inner file removed, the root rule still ignores saved sessions.

Checks:
- Failing precondition: against a local server returning 503, precondition failed and the other 6 tests didn't run.
- Repeated setup: running precondition and auth 10 times each gave 60 passed. A plain --repeat-each skips setup projects, so I selected them directly with --no-deps.

What I found on SauceDemo:
- Sessions last 10 minutes. The login cookie expires 600 seconds after it's set, which answers the question the framework-layout brainstorm had left open. A saved session only works for about 10 minutes
  after the run that created it. That also limits --no-deps debugging to about 10 minutes after a full run.
- performance_glitch_user is slow to log in. The Login click takes about 5 seconds, which fits under our 10-second action limit.

I updated CLAUDE.md to describe the three projects, both .auth/ folders and the 10-minute limit, and refreshed docs/prompt-log.txt.

The API app's check is still called health.setup.ts in a project named setup. I can rename it to precondition so both apps match.

ype Post`), `*.builder.ts` (`buildNewPost()`), `*.setup.ts` (setup projects only, always in `tests/setup/`), `*.spec.ts` (test projects only). Files in `utils/` have no suffix (`env.ts`, `

ests), lint and typecheck are clean, and the API app still passes. Nothing is committed or pushed.

**How the UI app runs now:** `precondition` → `auth` → `chromium`. Each project runs only if the one before it passed.
- **`tests/precondition.setup.ts`:** checks that the base URL responds. I used `expect(response).toBeOK()`, which is Playwright's assertion for `response.ok()` and matches the API app's h
ealth check. When it fails it shows the status code, e.g. "← 503 Service Unavailable". More startup checks can go in this file later.
- **`tests/auth.setup.ts`:** logs in each user through the login page, in parallel, and saves every session to `apps/ui/.auth/<username>.json`. That covers five users: `locked_out_user` i
s refused at the login form, so it has no session to save. The list is `SESSION_USERS` in `sauce-user.model.ts`.
- **`utils/auth.ts`:** holds `createStorageStateUI` and `storageStatePath`. I renamed the old `storage-state.ts` rather than keeping two files for the same thing (the rename is staged). I
 didn't use `auth.utils.ts` because files in `utils/` have no role suffix (`env.ts`, `matchers.ts`), and CLAUDE.md now says so.

A spec can run as another user with `test.use({ storageState: storageStatePath(SAUCE_USERS.problem) })`.

**`.auth/` in both apps:** each folder has its own `.gitignore` that ignores everything except itself, so the folders stay in git. I also changed the root rule from `.auth/` to `**/.auth/
*`, since the old rule hid the whole folder and those `.gitignore` files couldn't be committed. With the inner file removed, the root rule still ignores saved sessions.

**Checks:**
- **Failing precondition:** against a local server returning 503, `precondition` failed and the other 6 tests didn't run.
- **Repeated setup:** running `precondition` and `auth` 10 times each gave 60 passed. A plain `--repeat-each` skips setup projects, so I selected them directly with `--no-deps`.

**What I found on SauceDemo:**
- **Sessions last 10 minutes.** The login cookie expires 600 seconds after it's set, which answers the question the framework-layout brainstorm had left open. A saved session only works f
or about 10 minutes after the run that created it. That also limits `--no-deps` debugging to about 10 minutes after a full run.
- **`performance_glitch_user` is slow to log in.** The Login click takes about 5 seconds, which fits under our 10-second action limit.

I updated CLAUDE.md to describe the three projects, both `.auth/` folders and the 10-minute limit, and refreshed `docs/prompt-log.txt`.

The API app's check is still called `health.setup.ts` in a project named `setup`. I can rename it to `precondition` so both apps match.

### Rules


  | Folder | Can import (same app only) | Never contains |
  |---|---|---|
  | `tests/` | `fixtures/`, `data-models/`, `utils/` | Selectors, URLs, credentials, `new SomePage(page)` |
  | `tests/`, `setup/` | `fixtures/`, `data-models/`, `utils/` | Selectors, URLs, credentials, `new SomePage(page)` |
  | `fixtures/` | `pages/` or `clients/`, `data-models/`, `utils/` | Test logic, or assertions beyond "setup worked" |
  | `pages/` (UI) | `components/`, `data-models/`, `utils/` | Assertions (except a small `expectLoaded()`), waits, sleeps |
  | `components/` (UI) | `data-models/`, `utils/` | Navigation, or knowledge of the page around them |

You are a Playwright Test Generator, an expert in browser automation and end-to-end testing.
Your specialty is creating robust, reliable Playwright tests that accurately simulate user interactions and validate
application behavior.

# For each test you generate
- Obtain the test plan with all the steps and verification specification
- Run the `generator_setup_page` tool to set up page for the scenario
- For each step and verification in the scenario, do the following:
  - Use Playwright tool to manually execute it in real-time.
  - Use the step description as the intent for each Playwright tool call.
- Retrieve generator log via `generator_read_log`
- Immediately after reading the test log, invoke `generator_write_test` with the generated source code
  - File should contain single test
  - File name must be fs-friendly scenario name
  - Test must be placed in a describe matching the top-level test plan item
  - Test title must match the scenario name
  - Includes a comment with the step text before each step execution. Do not duplicate comments if step requires
    multiple actions.
  - Always use best practices from the log when generating tests.

---
name: playwright-test-healer
description: Use this agent when you need to debug and fix failing Playwright tests
tools: Glob, Grep, Read, LS, Edit, MultiEdit, Write, mcp__playwright-test__browser_console_messages, mcp__playwright-test__browser_evaluate, mcp__playwright-test__browser_generate_locator,

---

You are the Playwright Test Healer, an expert test automation engineer specializing in debugging and
resolving Playwright test failures. Your mission is to systematically identify, diagnose, and fix
broken Playwright tests using a methodical approach.

Your workflow:
1. **Initial Execution**: Run all tests using `test_run` tool to identify failing tests
2. **Debug failed tests**: For each failing test run `test_debug`.
3. **Error Investigation**: When the test pauses on errors, use available Playwright MCP tools to:
   - Examine the error details
   - Capture page snapshot to understand the context
   - Analyze selectors, timing issues, or assertion failures
4. **Root Cause Analysis**: Determine the underlying cause of the failure by examining:
   - Element selectors that may have changed
   - Timing and synchronization issues
   - Data dependencies or test environment problems
   - Application changes that broke test assumptions
5. **Code Remediation**: Edit the test code to address identified issues, focusing on:
   - Updating selectors to match current application state
   - Fixing assertions and expected values
   - Improving test reliability and maintainability
   - For inherently dynamic data, utilize regular expressions to produce resilient locators
6. **Verification**: Restart the test after each fix to validate the changes
7. **Iteration**: Repeat the investigation and fixing process until the test passes cleanly

Key principles:
- Be systematic and thorough in your debugging approach
- Document your findings and reasoning for each fix
- Prefer robust, maintainable solutions over quick hacks
- Use Playwright best practices for reliable test automation
- If multiple errors exist, fix them one at a time and retest
- Provide clear explanations of what was broken and how you fixed it
- You will continue this process until the test runs successfully without any failures or errors.

⏺ Update(CLAUDE.md)
Added 12 lines
- `npx playwright test -c apps/ui --project=chromium --no-deps` skips the setup projects and reuses the saved sessions, for local debugging. It only works within 10 minutes of the last fu
ll run.
- `npx playwright show-report apps/ui/playwright-report` opens an app's last report.

### Playwright test agents

UI tests are written with the official [Playwright test agents](https://playwright.dev/docs/test-agents). They drive a browser, so they work on `apps/ui` only.

- `playwright-test-planner` explores the site and saves a Markdown test plan to `specs/`.
- `playwright-test-generator` turns a plan scenario into a spec in `apps/ui/tests/`. It can only write inside a project's `testDir`.
- `playwright-test-healer` debugs failing tests and fixes them, or marks a test `test.fixme()` when the site itself is broken.
- The agents call the `playwright-test` MCP server from `.mcp.json`, which runs `npx playwright run-test-mcp-server -c apps/ui`. Without `-c apps/ui` it loads the root config and finds no
 tests.
- The planner and generator start from `apps/ui/tests/seed.spec.ts`. It runs in `chromium`, so the setup projects run first and the page opens logged in as `standard_user` on the inventor
y page.
- A generated spec is a draft. Before a PR, bring it in line with the rules above (fixture imports, locators and actions in page objects) and validate it like any new test.
- After a Playwright upgrade, regenerate the agents with `npx playwright init-agents --loop=claude -c apps/ui --project=chromium`. It overwrites the agent files and `.mcp.json`, so add `"
-c", "apps/ui"` back to the server args. It keeps the existing seed and `specs/`.

## Branches and pull requests

- Never commit directly to `main`. Every change goes through a branch and a pull request.

#### 6.1. Observations (informational only — not an automated test scenario)

**File:** `apps/ui/tests/auth.spec.ts`

**Steps:**
  1. This suite is not a real test.describe block to automate. It records observations made while exploring the app that were surprising or worth flagging to the team, gathered from manua
l verification in the browser:
    - expect: `performance_glitch_user` takes noticeably longer to log in than the other accounts (observed roughly 5-6 seconds versus ~1 second for others) — give it a generous timeout r
ather than treating the delay as flakiness.
    - expect: Both the Username and Password inputs are marked with the red error state and X-circle icon on every validation failure, even when only one of the two fields is actually the
 problem (e.g. submitting with only the username missing still marks the password field as errored too). This looks like a minor UI quirk rather than intentional design.
    - expect: Direct-URL protected-page errors ignore the query string: opening /inventory-item.html?id=4 while logged out produces an error naming only '/inventory-item.html', with the '
?id=4' silently dropped.
    - expect: The app does not reactively watch the session cookie: removing `session-username` while a protected page is already rendered does not immediately redirect the user. Only the
 next navigation (a reload or clicking any in-app link) is blocked, because every internal link performs a full page load rather than a client-side route change — so the cookie is effecti
vely re-checked on every navigation, not continuously.
    - expect: Dismissing an error alert (the 'Dismiss error' button) clears both the alert text and the red/error styling + icon on the inputs, but never clears whatever the user had alre
ady typed into Username or Password.
    - expect: Both the username and the password are case-sensitive; a correct username or password typed with different letter casing is rejected with the same generic 'do not match' mes
sage as a wholly wrong credential.
    - expect: The exact same generic message, "Epic sadface: Username and password do not match any user in this service", is reused for a wrong password, an unknown username, and case-mi
smatched credentials — the app never reveals which field or reason caused the failure, which is good practice for not helping credential-guessing.
    - expect: The session appears to live only in the `session-username` cookie; no authentication-related data was found in localStorage during these checks (localStorage only held unrel
ated analytics/backtrace keys).
- `performance_glitch_user` takes noticeably longer to log in than the other accounts (roughly 5-6 seconds versus about 1 second). The delay is inside the Login click, which stays within
the configured 10-second action timeout, so no special timeout is needed.
- Both the Username and Password inputs get the red error state and an X-circle icon on every validation failure, even when only one field is the problem. This looks like a minor UI quirk
. It is styling only (CSS classes and icons with no accessible name), so the tests don't assert on it.
- Direct-URL protected-page errors ignore the query string: opening /inventory-item.html?id=4 while logged out names only '/inventory-item.html'.
- The app does not watch the session cookie: removing `session-username` while a protected page is already shown does not redirect right away. Only the next navigation (a reload or any in
-app link, all of which are full page loads) is blocked.
- Dismissing an error clears the message and the error styling on the inputs, but keeps whatever was typed into Username and Password. A failed login also keeps the typed values.
- Username and password are both case-sensitive. A case mismatch gets the same generic "do not match" message as a wrong credential.
- The same message, "Epic sadface: Username and password do not match any user in this service", is used for a wrong password, an unknown username and case mismatches, so the app never re
veals which field was wrong.
- The session lives only in the `session-username` cookie. localStorage held only unrelated analytics/backtrace keys.

What I changed in the planner's draft:
- Removed checks on styling and cookies: the red input outline, the ✖ icons and whether the cookie exists. Our guides say to test what the user sees.
- One behavior per test: trimmed extra checks such as the page title and logo from the login test. I also split the logout scenario in two: landing back on the login page, and the inventory page being
  blocked afterwards.
- Expired session: rewrote the cookie scenario as "expired session is sent to login on the next page load". The planner's version checked that the page stays on screen until you navigate, which is a
  quirk of the app rather than behavior worth testing.
- No special timeout: removed the advice to give performance_glitch_user a longer timeout. Its ~5 second delay already fits our 10-second action limit.
- Notes: the planner has no way to add free-form notes, so it put them in a fake "test group". I made that a normal Notes section. It records a few quirks it found: the query string left out of the error
  message, both inputs showing an error when only one field is wrong, and typed values kept after an error.

The plan never includes the actual password.

⏺ Write(apps/ui/pages/login.page.ts)
Added 11 lines, removed 3 lines
import type { Locator, Page } from '@playwright/test';
import type { SauceUser } from '@ui/data-models/sauce-user.model';

export class LoginPage {
  readonly path = '/';
  readonly username: Locator;
  readonly password: Locator;
  readonly submit: Locator;
  readonly error: Locator;
  readonly dismissError: Locator;

  constructor(private readonly page: Page) {
    this.username = page.getByPlaceholder('Username');
    this.password = page.getByPlaceholder('Password');
    this.submit = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Login' });
    this.error = page.getByRole('alert');
    this.dismissError = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Dismiss error' });
  }

  async goto(): Promise<void> {
    await this.page.goto('/');
    await this.page.goto(this.path);
  }

  async login(username: SauceUser, password: string): Promise<void> {
  async fillCredentials(username: string, password: string): Promise<void> {
    await this.username.fill(username);
    await this.password.fill(password);
  }

  async login(username: string, password: string): Promise<void> {
    await this.fillCredentials(username, password);
    await this.submit.click();
  }
}

// spec: specs/auth.md
// seed: apps/ui/tests/seed.spec.ts

import { test, expect } from '@ui/fixtures/pages.fixture';

test.describe('Logout', () => {
  test('Going back in history after logout does not restore the protected page', async ({ page }) => {
    const openMenuButton = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Open Menu' });
    const loginButton = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Login' });
    const error = page.getByTestId('error');

    // 1. Starting state: logged in as standard_user, at https://www.saucedemo.com/inventory.html. Open the side
    // menu and click button "Logout".
    await page.goto('/inventory.html');
    await openMenuButton.click();
    await page.getByTestId('logout-sidebar-link').click();

    await expect(page).toHaveURL('/');
    await expect(loginButton).toBeVisible();
    await expect(error).not.toBeVisible();

    // 2. Trigger a browser back navigation (history back) to return to the previously visited /inventory.html entry.
    await page.goBack();

    await expect(page).toHaveURL('/');
    await expect(error).toHaveText("Epic sadface: You can only access '/inventory.html' when you are logged in.");
  });
});

t's every user except `locked_out_user`, which SauceDemo refuses. SauceDemo has no login API, so the UI is the only way to get a session.
    - `chromium` runs the specs logged in as `standard_user`. A spec switches user with `test.use({ storageState: storageStatePath(SAUCE_USERS.problem) })`, and login tests opt out with `t
est.use({ storageState: { cookies: [], origins: [] } })`.
    - `chromium` runs the specs logged in as `standard_user`. A spec switches user with `test.use({ storageState: storageStatePath(SAUCE_USERS.problem) })`, and tests that start logged out
 use `test.use({ storageState: emptyStorageState })`.
    - A SauceDemo session lasts 10 minutes (the `session-username` cookie), so a saved session only outlives the run that made it by a few minutes.
  - API: `setup` (`setup/health.setup.ts`) checks that the API responds, then `api` runs the specs.
- Each app writes its own `test-results/` and `playwright-report/` inside its folder. Keep `outputDir` and the reporter output paths explicit in `baseConfig`. Left to Playwright's defaults
, both apps would write to the repo root and overwrite each other.

- File names are kebab-case with a role suffix: `*.page.ts` (`InventoryPage`), `*.component.ts` (`HeaderComponent`), `*.api.ts` (`PostsApi`), `*.fixture.ts`, `*.model.ts` (`postSchema`, `
type Post`), `*.builder.ts` (`buildNewPost()`), `*.setup.ts` (setup projects only, always in `setup/`), `*.spec.ts` (test projects only). Files in `utils/` have no suffix (`env.ts`, `auth
.ts`).
- File names are kebab-case with a role suffix: `*.page.ts` (`InventoryPage`), `*.component.ts` (`HeaderComponent`), `*.api.ts` (`PostsApi`), `*.fixture.ts`, `*.model.ts` (`postSchema`, `
type Post`), `*.builder.ts` (`buildNewPost()`), `*.oracle.ts` (`PRODUCTS`, the expected data for a UI feature, always in `data-models/`), `*.setup.ts` (setup projects only, always in `set
up/`), `*.spec.ts` (test projects only). Files in `utils/` have no suffix (`env.ts`, `auth.ts`).

### Expected data: synthetic oracles

UI tests treat SauceDemo's data as synthetic: they're written as if the test automation had seeded the store, so every expected value is known before the test runs and never read from the
 page.

- A feature's expected data lives in an oracle, `apps/ui/data-models/<feature>.oracle.ts`. For example, `product-catalog.oracle.ts` holds every product's id, name, description, price and
image, and the order each sort option gives.
- Write what the store is **meant** to show. SauceDemo plants mistakes on purpose for testers to find (typos, wrong prices or images, broken sorting or buttons for some users). Never copy
 a value from the site into an oracle without checking that it's right, and never write an expected value that matches a mistake.
- Derive expected results from the oracle's data and rules, for example sort orders from sorting the oracle's products, instead of lists copied from the page.
- Match only what the store controls. For example, the build adds a hash to image file names, so match the stable part of the name.
- When the site differs from an oracle, the test fails, and that failure is the finding. Don't make it pass by editing the oracle, loosening the assertion, or marking the test `test.fixme
()`, `test.fail()` or `test.skip()`. Record the finding in the notes of the feature's plan in `specs/`.

### Adding code



- `playwright-test-planner` explores the site and saves a Markdown test plan to `specs/`.
- `playwright-test-generator` turns a plan scenario into a spec in `apps/ui/tests/`. It can only write inside a project's `testDir`.
- `playwright-test-healer` debugs failing tests and fixes them, or marks a test `test.fixme()` when the site itself is broken.
- `playwright-test-healer` debugs failing tests and fixes them. A test that fails because the site differs from an oracle is a finding, not a broken test: the healer fixes only the test c
ode (locators, steps) and leaves the oracle, the assertion and the failure alone.
- The agents call the `playwright-test` MCP server from `.mcp.json`, which runs `npx playwright run-test-mcp-server -c apps/ui`. Without `-c apps/ui` it loads the root config and finds no
 tests.
- The planner and generator start from `apps/ui/tests/seed.spec.ts`. It runs in `chromium`, so the setup projects run first and the page opens logged in as `standard_user` on the inventor
y page.
- A generated spec is a draft. Before a PR, bring it in line with the rules above (fixture imports, locators and actions in page objects) and validate it like any new test.
Updated README.md (+11 -1)
# VNDNT
# VNDNT

Playwright and TypeScript tests for SauceDemo (UI) and JSONPlaceholder (API).

## Test data: synthetic oracles

The UI tests treat SauceDemo's data as synthetic. They're written as if the test automation had seeded the store, so every expected value is known before a test runs.

- The expected data lives in oracle files, `apps/ui/data-models/*.oracle.ts`. `product-catalog.oracle.ts` holds each product's id, name, description, price and image. The expected sort ord
ers are derived from those values.
- An oracle holds what the store is meant to show, not what the site renders today. SauceDemo plants mistakes on purpose for testers to find, so a mistake is never copied into an oracle.
- When the site differs from an oracle, the test fails. That failure is a finding: report it, and don't change the oracle, loosen the assertion or skip the test to make it pass.

⏺ Write(apps/ui/pages/inventory.page.ts)
Added 27 lines
import type { Locator, Page } from '@playwright/test';
import { HeaderComponent } from '@ui/components/header.component';
import { ProductCardComponent } from '@ui/components/product-card.component';

export class InventoryPage {
  readonly path = '/inventory.html';
  readonly header: HeaderComponent;
  readonly title: Locator;
  readonly items: Locator;
  readonly itemNames: Locator;
  readonly itemPrices: Locator;
  readonly sortSelect: Locator;
  // The sort dropdown's visible label
  readonly activeSort: Locator;

  constructor(private readonly page: Page) {
    this.header = new HeaderComponent(page.getByTestId('primary-header'));
    this.title = page.getByTestId('title');
    this.items = page.getByTestId('inventory-item');
    this.itemNames = this.items.getByTestId('inventory-item-name');
    this.itemPrices = this.items.getByTestId('inventory-item-price');
    this.sortSelect = page.getByRole('combobox', { name: 'Sort products' });
    this.activeSort = page.getByTestId('active-option');
  }

  async goto(): Promise<void> {
    await this.page.goto(this.path);
  }

  // The card with exactly this product name
  product(name: string): ProductCardComponent {
    return new ProductCardComponent(
      this.items.filter({ has: this.page.getByTestId('inventory-item-name').getByText(name, { exact: true }) }),
    );
  }

  // Every card on the page right now, top to bottom. It doesn't wait: assert the item count first.
  async productCards(): Promise<ProductCardComponent[]> {
    const items = await this.items.all();
    return items.map((item) => new ProductCardComponent(item));
  }

  async sortBy(label: string): Promise<void> {
    await this.sortSelect.selectOption({ label });
  }
}

import { test, expect } from '@ui/fixtures/pages.fixture';

test.describe('Add to cart', () => {
  test('Add to cart state is shared between the product list and its details page', async ({
    page,
    inventoryPage,
  }) => {
    // 1. Starting state: logged in as standard_user, empty cart. Navigate to /inventory.html and click button "Add to cart" on the Sauce Labs Onesie card.
    await inventoryPage.goto();
    const cartBadge = page.getByTestId('shopping-cart-badge');

    const card = page.getByTestId('inventory-item').filter({ hasText: 'Sauce Labs Onesie' });
    const listAddToCartButton = card.getByRole('button', { name: 'Add to cart' });
    await listAddToCartButton.click();

    await expect(card.getByRole('button', { name: 'Remove' })).toBeVisible();
    await expect(cartBadge).toHaveText('1');

    // 2. Open Sauce Labs Onesie's details page (its name or image link, URL ?id=2).
    await card.getByTestId('item-2-title-link').click();

    await expect(page).toHaveURL('/inventory-item.html?id=2');
    const detailsRemoveButton = page.getByTestId('remove');
    await expect(detailsRemoveButton).toBeVisible();
    await expect(cartBadge).toHaveText('1');

    // 3. Click "Remove" on the details page, then click "Back to products".
    await detailsRemoveButton.click();
    await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Back to products' }).click();

    await expect(page).toHaveURL('/inventory.html');
    await expect(cartBadge).toBeHidden();
    await expect(listAddToCartButton).toBeVisible();
  });
});

⏺ Write(specs/product-catalog.md)
Added 107 lines, removed 164 lines

## Application Overview

SauceDemo (https://www.saucedemo.com) product catalog: the inventory page (`/inventory.html`) lists 6 products with a sort dropdown, and each product has a details page (`/inventory-item.h
tml?id=N`) reachable by clicking either the product's image or its name. All scenarios below run **logged in as standard_user**, start with an **empty cart**, and start on `/inventory.html
` unless a scenario says otherwise (clear the cart with `localStorage.removeItem('cart-contents')` if a previous run left items in it).
SauceDemo (https://www.saucedemo.com) product catalog: the inventory page (`/inventory.html`) lists 6 products with a sort dropdown, and each product has a details page (`/inventory-item.h
tml?id=N`) that opens from either the product's image or its name. Opening a product is a client-side navigation: the URL changes before the details page renders, and the list and the deta
ils page use the same `data-test` names for a product's name, description, price and cart button.

The data below is treated as the catalog's **intended** data, not just whatever the site currently renders — SauceDemo deliberately plants defects on some accounts, so expected results des
cribe the intended catalog and any deviation is called out in Notes instead of being baked into a scenario as if it were correct.
All scenarios run **logged in as standard_user**, start with an **empty cart**, and start on `/inventory.html` unless a scenario says otherwise.

**Products (intended data, standard_user), in default list order:**
**Synthetic data.** The catalog is treated as data the test automation seeded, so the expected values below are what the store is **meant** to show, not whatever the site renders. SauceDem
o plants mistakes on purpose, so no observed value is copied into an expected result without checking it. When the site differs, the test fails and the difference is a finding (see Notes).
 The tests read these values from the oracle, `apps/ui/data-models/product-catalog.oracle.ts`.

| id | Name | Price | Image file | Image alt |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | Sauce Labs Backpack | $29.99 | sauce-backpack-1200x1500-CjRW-Djj.jpg | Sauce Labs Backpack |
| 0 | Sauce Labs Bike Light | $9.99 | bike-light-1200x1500-DxcZRFOA.jpg | Sauce Labs Bike Light |
| 1 | Sauce Labs Bolt T-Shirt | $15.99 | bolt-shirt-1200x1500-mR0ldpVS.jpg | Sauce Labs Bolt T-Shirt |
| 5 | Sauce Labs Fleece Jacket | $49.99 | sauce-pullover-1200x1500-BfbI-PSd.jpg | Sauce Labs Fleece Jacket |
| 2 | Sauce Labs Onesie | $7.99 | red-onesie-1200x1500-BrSuq0ic.jpg | Sauce Labs Onesie |
| 3 | Test.allTheThings() T-Shirt (Red) | $15.99 | red-tatt-1200x1500-E-qp6aYf.jpg | Test.allTheThings() T-Shirt (Red) |
**Products (intended data), in the default order, Name (A to Z):**

This default order equals the "Name (A to Z)" sort. Every product's list entry, details page, image and Add to cart control was checked against this table for `standard_user` and no discre
pancy was found: names, descriptions, prices and images are consistent between the list and the details page for all 6 products, and all four sort options genuinely reorder the list by nam
e or by price with a stable tie-break (see the Sorting section). The `?id=999` details page is a deliberate "not found" easter egg (joke copy, image `sl-404-Cq1a9k9X.jpg`, price `$√-1`) ra
ther than a data defect, so its content is documented as-is in the Product details section.
| id | Name | Price | Image file (without the build hash) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | Sauce Labs Backpack | $29.99 | sauce-backpack-1200x1500 |
| 0 | Sauce Labs Bike Light | $9.99 | bike-light-1200x1500 |
| 1 | Sauce Labs Bolt T-Shirt | $15.99 | bolt-shirt-1200x1500 |
| 5 | Sauce Labs Fleece Jacket | $49.99 | sauce-pullover-1200x1500 |
| 2 | Sauce Labs Onesie | $7.99 | red-onesie-1200x1500 |
| 3 | Test.allTheThings() T-Shirt (Red) | $15.99 | red-tatt-1200x1500 |

Two structural notes useful when locating elements: (1) both the image and the name of a product render as separate elements sharing the exact same accessible name "View details for `<Name
>`" (image first in the DOM, name second) — there is no way to tell them apart by accessible name alone. (2) Every list card's Add to cart/Remove button has the accessible name "Add to car
t"/"Remove" only, with no product name in it; the product is only identifiable by the card it's in or by its `data-test` attribute (e.g. `add-to-cart-sauce-labs-backpack`). The details pag
e's Add to cart/Remove button similarly has `data-test="add-to-cart"`/`"remove"` with no product suffix.
Every image's alt text is the product name.

**Notes — deviations found on other accounts (informational only, not covered by scenarios; all scenarios run as standard_user only):**
**Descriptions (intended data):**

- **problem_user**: every product on the *list* page shows the same generic placeholder image (`/assets/sl-404-Cq1a9k9X.jpg`, the "item not found" image) instead of its real photo, even th
ough each image's `alt` text still correctly names the product; the *details* page image is correct. The sort dropdown is non-functional: selecting "Name (Z to A)", "Price (low to high)" o
r "Price (high to low)" leaves the `<select>` value and the list order unchanged at "Name (A to Z)". Add to cart on the details page works normally (verified on Sauce Labs Backpack); not s
eparately re-verified on the list.
- **error_user**: list and details data match the intended catalog (verified list only). Clicking "Add to cart" for **Sauce Labs Bolt T-Shirt** specifically fails silently: the button stay
s "Add to cart", the header cart badge does not increment, and the browser console logs `Error: Failed to add item to the cart.` plus a blocked CORS request to `submit.backtrace.io`. Other
 products (verified: Sauce Labs Backpack) add to cart normally. Changing the sort dropdown to any option shows a native `alert()` reading "Sorting is broken! This error has been reported t
o Backtrace." instead of resorting the list.
- **visual_user**: list prices do not match the intended catalog (observed in one session: Sauce Labs Backpack $89.94, Sauce Labs Bike Light $69.05, Sauce Labs Bolt T-Shirt $11.83, Sauce L
abs Fleece Jacket $83.87, Sauce Labs Onesie $42.62, Test.allTheThings() T-Shirt (Red) $47.92). The Sauce Labs Backpack list image is swapped for the generic "item not found" placeholder wh
ile the other 5 products show their correct images. Not re-checked on the details page or after sorting/add to cart.
- **performance_glitch_user**: list and details data match the intended catalog exactly; the only observed difference is noticeably slower page/action responses, not a catalog data defect.
- Sauce Labs Backpack: "carry.allTheThings() with the sleek, streamlined Sly Pack that melds uncompromising style with unequaled laptop and tablet protection."
- Sauce Labs Bike Light: "A red light isn't the desired state in testing but it sure helps when riding your bike at night. Water-resistant with 3 lighting modes, 1 AAA battery included."
- Sauce Labs Bolt T-Shirt: "Get your testing superhero on with the Sauce Labs bolt T-shirt. From American Apparel, 100% ringspun combed cotton, heather gray with red bolt."
- Sauce Labs Fleece Jacket: "It's not every day that you come across a midweight quarter-zip fleece jacket capable of handling everything from a relaxing day outdoors to a busy day at the
office."
- Sauce Labs Onesie: "Rib snap infant onesie for the junior automation engineer in development. Reinforced 3-snap bottom closure, two-needle hemmed sleeves and bottom won't unravel."
- Test.allTheThings() T-Shirt (Red): "This classic Sauce Labs t-shirt is perfect to wear when cozying up to your keyboard to automate a few tests. Super-soft and comfy ringspun combed cott
on."

| Option | Names, top to bottom |
|---|---|
| Name (A to Z) | Backpack, Bike Light, Bolt T-Shirt, Fleece Jacket, Onesie, Test.allTheThings() T-Shirt (Red) |

| Price (high to low) | Fleece Jacket $49.99, Backpack $29.99, Bolt T-Shirt $15.99, Test.allTheThings() T-Shirt (Red) $15.99, Bike Light $9.99, Onesie $7.99 |

T-Shirt (Red).

#### 4.7. Adding several different products updates the cart badge and each product's own button independently
#### 4.8. Every product can be added, and the badge counts them

**File:** `apps/ui/tests/product-catalog.spec.ts`

**Steps:**
  1. Starting state: logged in as standard_user, empty cart. Navigate to https://www.saucedemo.com/inventory.html.
  2. Click "Add to cart" on Sauce Labs Backpack, then on Sauce Labs Bike Light, then on Sauce Labs Fleece Jacket, one after another, checking state after each click.
    - expect: After Sauce Labs Backpack: its button reads "Remove"; header cart button reads "Cart, 1 items".
    - expect: After Sauce Labs Bike Light: its button reads "Remove"; header cart button reads "Cart, 2 items".
    - expect: After Sauce Labs Fleece Jacket: its button reads "Remove"; header cart button reads "Cart, 3 items".
    - expect: Sauce Labs Bolt T-Shirt, Sauce Labs Onesie and Test.allTheThings() T-Shirt (Red), never clicked, still read "Add to cart".
  3. Reload the page by navigating to https://www.saucedemo.com/inventory.html again.
    - expect: The header cart button still reads "Cart, 3 items".

Updated CLAUDE.md (+1 -0)
- **Apps never import from each other.** Nothing in `apps/ui` imports `@api/*`, and nothing in `apps/api` imports `@ui/*`. If both apps ever need the same helper, add a root `shared/` fold
er at that point.
- Import within an app through its alias (`@ui/...`, `@api/...`). App config files use relative imports.
- Specs import `test` and `expect` from their app's fixture file, not from `@playwright/test`.
- Don't tag tests (`{ tag: '@smoke' }`). To run part of a suite, pick an app, a project or a spec file.
- Read environment variables only through the app's `utils/env.ts`, and add every new variable to `.env.example`.
- Layers within an app. Imports only go down this table:

Updated docs/guidelines/general.md (+2 -9)
## 5. Organization and execution

- Split UI and API tests into separate Playwright **projects**, so either can run alone (`--project=api`).
- Tag tests for selective runs, for example `@smoke` for a fast critical-path check before the full suite.

  ```ts
  test('user can check out', { tag: '@smoke' }, async ({ page }) => { ... });
  // npx playwright test --grep @smoke
  ```

- Use `test.skip(condition, 'reason')` and `test.fixme('reason')` with a reason, and link a ticket when one exists. Never comment tests out.
- Set `forbidOnly: !!process.env.CI` so a stray `test.only` can't slip into CI.



## 10. CI

- Run tests on every pull request. Run the smoke set first so failures show up fast.
- Run tests on every pull request.
- Install only the browsers you use: `npx playwright install chromium --with-deps`.
- Shard large suites across machines (`--shard=1/4`) and merge the blob reports.
- Keep `@playwright/test` up to date. New versions ship newer browsers and fixes.

- [ ] No sleeps, arbitrary timeouts or logic in the test body
- [ ] No raw selectors, URLs or secrets in the spec
- [ ] Assertions are specific, and you've seen them fail
- [ ] It's tagged and sits in the right project
- [ ] It sits in the right project
Updated docs/guidelines/ui-tests.md (+1 -1)

## 9. Browsers, viewports and visual checks

- Choose browser coverage on purpose: run the full suite on Chromium and a smoke set on Firefox and WebKit, or everything everywhere if the suite is fast enough. Use `projects` and `devic
es['Pixel 7']`-style presets for mobile emulation.
- Choose browser coverage on purpose: run the full suite on every browser you support, or only on Chromium if the product doesn't need more. Use `projects` and `devices['Pixel 7']`-style
presets for mobile emulation.
- Visual comparisons (`toHaveScreenshot`) are powerful but brittle. Mask dynamic content (`mask: [...]`), generate baselines in the same OS and browser as CI (for example with the Playwri
ght Docker image) and use them only where layout really matters.

## 10. Accessibility

⏺ Write(apps/ui/components/header.component.ts)

export class HeaderComponent {
  readonly menuButton: Locator;
  readonly logoutButton: Locator;
  readonly resetAppStateButton: Locator;
  readonly cartLink: Locator;
  // The item count on the cart icon. It's gone while the cart is empty.
  readonly cartBadge: Locator;

  constructor(root: Locator) {
    this.menuButton = root.getByRole('button', { name: 'Open Menu' });
    // A link with role="button". The closed menu is aria-hidden, so it only matches once the menu is open.
    // Menu items are links with role="button". The closed menu is aria-hidden, so they only match once it's open.
    this.logoutButton = root.getByRole('button', { name: 'Logout' });
    this.resetAppStateButton = root.getByRole('button', { name: 'Reset App State' });
    // Its accessible name changes with the count ("Cart, empty", "Cart, 2 items"), so match it by data-test
    this.cartLink = root.getByTestId('shopping-cart-link');
    this.cartBadge = root.getByTestId('shopping-cart-badge');
  }


    await this.menuButton.click();
    await this.logoutButton.click();
  }

  async resetAppState(): Promise<void> {
    await this.menuButton.click();
    await this.resetAppStateButton.click();
  }
}

⏺ Write(apps/ui/fixtures/pages.fixture.ts)
Added 31 lines, removed 1 line
import { test as base } from '@playwright/test';
import type { Product } from '@ui/data-models/product-catalog.oracle';
import { CartPage } from '@ui/pages/cart.page';
import { CheckoutStepOnePage } from '@ui/pages/checkout-step-one.page';
import { InventoryItemPage } from '@ui/pages/inventory-item.page';
import { InventoryPage } from '@ui/pages/inventory.page';
import { LoginPage } from '@ui/pages/login.page';
import { env } from '@ui/utils/env';

type PageFixtures = {
  loginPage: LoginPage;
  inventoryPage: InventoryPage;
  inventoryItemPage: InventoryItemPage;
  cartPage: CartPage;
  checkoutStepOnePage: CheckoutStepOnePage;
};

export const test = base.extend<PageFixtures>({
type StateFixtures = {
  // Puts these products in the cart, in this order, without the UI. Call it before the test opens a page.
  fillCart: (products: Product[]) => Promise<void>;
};

export const test = base.extend<PageFixtures & StateFixtures>({
  loginPage: async ({ page }, use) => {
    await use(new LoginPage(page));
  },

  inventoryItemPage: async ({ page }, use) => {
    await use(new InventoryItemPage(page));
  },
  cartPage: async ({ page }, use) => {
    await use(new CartPage(page));
  },
  checkoutStepOnePage: async ({ page }, use) => {
    await use(new CheckoutStepOnePage(page));
  },
  fillCart: async ({ context }, use) => {
    await use(async (products) => {
      // SauceDemo keeps the cart only in localStorage: cart-contents is a JSON array of product ids, in the order they
      // were added. setStorageState replaces the whole state, so pass the session cookie back in with it.
      const origin = new URL(env.UI_BASE_URL).origin;
      const cart = { name: 'cart-contents', value: JSON.stringify(products.map(({ id }) => id)) };
      const state = await context.storageState();
      await context.setStorageState({
        ...state,
        origins: [...state.origins.filter((entry) => entry.origin !== origin), { origin, localStorage: [cart] }],
      });

    - `precondition` (`setup/precondition.setup.ts`) checks that the base URL responds. Add any other check that must pass before a login here.
    - `auth` (`setup/auth.setup.ts`) logs in every user in `SESSION_USERS` through the login page and saves each session with `createStorageStateUI` to `apps/ui/.auth/<username>.json`. Tha
t's every user except `locked_out_user`, which SauceDemo refuses. SauceDemo has no login API, so the UI is the only way to get a session.
    - `chromium` runs the specs logged in as `standard_user`. A spec switches user with `test.use({ storageState: storageStatePath(SAUCE_USERS.problem) })`, and tests that start logged out
 use `test.use({ storageState: emptyStorageState })`.
    - SauceDemo keeps the cart only in localStorage. A test that needs products in the cart calls `fillCart([CATALOG.backpack, ...])` before opening a page, instead of clicking Add to cart
. Click through the UI only when adding or removing is what the test checks.
    - A SauceDemo session lasts 10 minutes (the `session-username` cookie), so a saved session only outlives the run that made it by a few minutes.
  - API: `setup` (`setup/health.setup.ts`) checks that the API responds, then `api` runs the specs.
- Each app writes its own `test-results/` and `playwright-report/` inside its folder. Keep `outputDir` and the reporter output paths explicit in `baseConfig`. Left to Playwright's defaults
, both apps would write to the repo root and overwrite each other.

import type { Locator, Page } from '@playwright/test';
import type { Buyer } from '@ui/data-models/checkout.model';

// The first checkout step, where the buyer enters their name and postal code
export class CheckoutStepOnePage {
  readonly path = '/checkout-step-one.html';
  readonly title: Locator;
  readonly firstName: Locator;
  readonly lastName: Locator;
  readonly postalCode: Locator;
  readonly continueButton: Locator;

  constructor(page: Page) {
    this.title = page.getByTestId('title');
    this.firstName = page.getByPlaceholder('First Name');
    this.lastName = page.getByPlaceholder('Last Name');
    this.postalCode = page.getByPlaceholder('Zip/Postal Code');
    this.continueButton = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Continue' });
  }

  async submitInformation(buyer: Buyer): Promise<void> {
    await this.firstName.fill(buyer.firstName);
    await this.lastName.fill(buyer.lastName);
    await this.postalCode.fill(buyer.postalCode);
    await this.continueButton.click();
  }
}

      checkoutStepOnePage,
      checkoutStepTwoPage,
    }) => {
      await fillCart(products);
      await cartPage.goto();
      await cartPage.checkoutButton.click();

      await checkoutStepOnePage.submitInformation(BUYER);

      const totals = cartTotals(products);
      await expect.soft(checkoutStepTwoPage.itemTotal).toHaveText(totals.itemTotal);
      await expect.soft(checkoutStepTwoPage.tax).toHaveText(totals.tax);
      await expect.soft(checkoutStepTwoPage.total).toHaveText(totals.total);
    });
  }
});

Updated CLAUDE.md (+1 -1)

UI tests treat SauceDemo's data as synthetic: they're written as if the test automation had seeded the store, so every expected value is known before the test runs and never read from the
 page.

- A feature's expected data lives in an oracle, `apps/ui/data-models/<feature>.oracle.ts`. For example, `product-catalog.oracle.ts` holds every product's id, name, description, price and
image, and the order each sort option gives.
- A feature's expected data lives in an oracle, `apps/ui/data-models/<feature>.oracle.ts`. For example, `product-catalog.oracle.ts` holds every product's id, name, description, price and
image, and the order each sort option gives, and `cart.oracle.ts` holds the tax rule and works out each cart's totals.
- Write what the store is **meant** to show. SauceDemo plants mistakes on purpose for testers to find (typos, wrong prices or images, broken sorting or buttons for some users). Never copy
 a value from the site into an oracle without checking that it's right, and never write an expected value that matches a mistake.
- Derive expected results from the oracle's data and rules, for example sort orders from sorting the oracle's products, instead of lists copied from the page.
- Match only what the store controls. For example, the build adds a hash to image file names, so match the stable part of the name.
Updated specs/cart.md (+28 -1)

**Cart page.** Title "Your Cart", column labels "QTY" and "Description", one row per product with quantity "1", the name as a link to the details page, the description, the price and a "Re
move" button, then "Continue Shopping" and "Checkout". The page shows no total.

**Totals.** The cart is first added up on the checkout overview (`/checkout-step-two.html`), after the buyer submits their name and postal code on `/checkout-step-one.html`. The store char
ges 8% tax on the item total, rounded to the nearest cent: "Item total: $X", "Tax: $Y", "Total: $X+Y". The expected totals come from the cart oracle, `apps/ui/data-models/cart.oracle.ts`,
which works in whole cents.

The catalog work is committed as da5bed3. The shopping cart tests are in apps/ui/tests/cart.spec.ts and not committed yet. The UI suite gives 76 passed and 7 failed, and all 7 failures are bugs in the
site, not in the tests.

Bugs the tests catch (they stay red):
- Wrong item total: the item total isn't rounded to cents. The 4-product cart shows Item total: $105.96000000000001. A separate script checked all 63 possible carts: 6 have this problem, and tax and
  total were right in all 63.
- Checkout with an empty cart: Checkout still opens the "Your Information" page, so the test expects the button to be disabled.
- Reset App State: it clears the badge but leaves the Remove buttons on the list and the rows on the cart page until you reload. That's 2 tests.
- Onesie typo: "sleeved" shows up on the cart page too. That's 1 test here, plus the 2 catalog tests that were already failing.

What cart.spec.ts covers (27 tests, as standard_user):
- Adding and removing: from the list and the details page, the two pages agreeing with each other, the badge counting each product up, the badge disappearing only after the last product is removed, and
  the cart surviving a reload. I moved the catalog spec's "Add to cart" tests here, so nothing is duplicated.
- Cart page:
  - it opens from the header link
  - each row's quantity, name, description and price match the oracle (a loop over the rows with one step per product)
  - only the added products appear, in the order they were added
  - an empty cart shows no products
- Removing on the cart page: the other rows keep their order, and the product shows "Add to cart" again on the list.
- Navigation: a row's name opens the product's details, Continue Shopping goes back to the list, and Checkout opens the first checkout step.
- Totals: carts of 1 to 6 products, each adding the next product in catalog order. Each test goes through the real checkout form and checks the item total, the 8% tax and the total. The new
  cart.oracle.ts computes them in whole cents.

How the tests set up a cart: a new fillCart([...]) fixture writes the cart into localStorage before the page loads. Only tests about adding or removing click the buttons. I also added cart and checkout
page objects and a cart row component, and gave the header the cart link and Reset App State.

Validation:
- Breaking the tests on purpose:
  - removing each click made every test fail except the empty-cart display test, which has no click
  - emptying the carts made every test that relies on fillCart fail
  - a 9% tax made every Tax and Total check fail
- Repeat run: with 10 repeats, only the bug tests failed, and nothing was flaky.
- Other checks: the API suite passed (2 tests), and lint and typecheck are clean. The prompt log is refreshed and doesn't contain the password.
- Docs: specs/cart.md now matches the tests and lists the findings. CLAUDE.md now describes fillCart and the cart oracle.

Left out on purpose (recorded in the plan's notes):
- Cart after logout: it survives logging out, but whether it should isn't defined.
- Hand-edited localStorage: an unknown id or a repeated id can't happen through the UI.
- Other users: problem_user's overview doubles the item total, and for error_user some products can't be added and none could be removed. Neither is tested, because you chose standard_user only.
- Checkout shortcut: the overview page opens directly without the name and postal code step. That belongs with the checkout tests.

⏺ Write(specs/checkout.md)
Added 131 lines, removed 91 lines

## Application Overview

SauceDemo's checkout runs across three pages reached from the cart's "Checkout" button (`specs/cart.md` 4.3): `/checkout-step-one.html` ("Checkout: Your Information") collects First Name,       Last Name and Zip/Postal Code with "Cancel" and "Continue"; `/checkout-step-two.html` ("Checkout: Overview") lists the cart, the store's fixed payment and shipping information, and the tot
als, with "Cancel" and "Finish"; `/checkout-complete.html` ("Checkout: Complete!") confirms the order with a "Back Home" button (and, once an order has actually been finished in the same s
ession, a "Generate PDF order" button). All three, like every protected page, redirect a logged-out visitor to `/` with a page-specific error; that's covered by `specs/auth.md` section 5 a
nd not repeated here.
SauceDemo (https://www.saucedemo.com) checkout runs across three pages, starting from "Checkout" on the cart page (`specs/cart.md` 4.3):

All scenarios run logged in as standard_user. The cart is set directly in localStorage (`cart-contents`, a JSON array of product ids in the order added: backpack=4, bikeLight=0, boltTShirt
=1, fleeceJacket=5, onesie=2, redTShirt=3 — see `specs/cart.md`) instead of clicking "Add to cart", unless a scenario is specifically about reaching checkout from the cart. Totals (8% tax
on the item total, rounded to the nearest cent) are computed once, for every cart size, in `specs/cart.md` 6.1 (the cart oracle, `apps/ui/data-models/cart.oracle.ts`); scenarios below that
 need a total reference it instead of repeating the maths. Products and prices come from the catalog oracle, `apps/ui/data-models/product-catalog.oracle.ts`.
- `/checkout-step-one.html`, "Checkout: Your Information": First Name, Last Name and Zip/Postal Code, with "Cancel" and "Continue". A missing field shows an alert with a "Dismiss error" bu
tton.
- `/checkout-step-two.html`, "Checkout: Overview": one row per product (quantity, name, description and price, with no "Remove" button), "Payment Information: SauceCard #31337", "Shipping
Information: Free Pony Express Delivery!" and the totals, with "Cancel" and "Finish".
- `/checkout-complete.html`, "Checkout: Complete!": the "Pony Express" image, the heading "Thank you for your order!", the text "Your order has been dispatched, and will arrive just as fas
t as the pony can get there!", "Back Home" and "Generate PDF order".

**Synthetic data.** As in `specs/cart.md` and `specs/product-catalog.md`, expected results describe what checkout is meant to do, not whatever the site does. SauceDemo plants mistakes on p
urpose. When the site differs, the scenario keeps the intended expectation and the difference is recorded as a Finding in `## Notes`.
Moving between the pages is a client-side navigation, so the URL changes before the new page renders.                                                                                           
## Notes
All scenarios run **logged in as standard_user**. Logged-out visitors are sent to the login page from every checkout page, which `specs/auth.md` section 5 covers.

Observations from exploring the app. They are not test scenarios.
**Synthetic data.** The store is treated as data the test automation seeded, so expected results describe what checkout is **meant** to do, not whatever the site does. Products and prices
come from the catalog oracle, `apps/ui/data-models/product-catalog.oracle.ts`, and the checkout texts, errors and incomplete-information cases from the checkout oracle, `apps/ui/data-model
s/checkout.oracle.ts`. When the site differs, the test fails and the difference is a finding (see Notes).

- **Finding:** `/checkout-step-one.html` opens directly even with an empty cart, instead of redirecting to `/cart.html`, so 4.1 fails.
- **Finding:** `/checkout-step-two.html` opens directly even with an empty cart, instead of redirecting to `/cart.html`, so 4.2 fails. It shows an all-zero total with an inconsistent forma
t: "Item total: $0" (no decimals), "Tax: $0.00", "Total: $0.00".
- **Finding:** The overview opens directly with a filled cart, skipping the name and postal code step entirely, instead of redirecting to `/checkout-step-one.html` (already noted for the t
otals scenarios in `specs/cart.md`), so 4.3 fails.
- **Finding:** `/checkout-complete.html` opens directly even with an empty cart, showing the same "Thank you for your order!" message as a real order, instead of redirecting to `/cart.html
`, so 4.4 fails.
- **Finding:** The confirmation page opens directly with a filled cart and no information submitted, and the cart keeps its products (the badge still shows the count) instead of the buyer
being sent to `/checkout-step-one.html` with no order placed, so 4.5 fails.
- **Finding:** The confirmation page opens directly after the information step but before "Finish", again without emptying the cart, instead of sending the buyer back to `/checkout-step-tw
o.html`, so 4.6 fails.
- Whichever way the confirmation page is reached without clicking "Finish" earlier in the same session (4.4, 4.5, 4.6), it shows only "Back Home", no "Generate PDF order": the order detail
s behind the PDF live in memory, not in localStorage or sessionStorage, so a fresh page load never has them.
- **Finding:** First Name, Last Name and Zip/Postal Code all accept a value of only spaces and continue to the overview instead of being treated as blank, so 3.3 fails for every field.
- Checkout's validation errors read "Error: <field> is required", unlike the login page's "Epic sadface: " prefix (`specs/auth.md`). The Zip/Postal Code field is also named just "Postal Co
de" in its error, dropping "Zip/".
- Deviations on other accounts (the scenarios run as standard_user only):
  - **problem_user:** typing into "Last Name" never updates it — the characters are applied to "First Name" instead (typing "Doe" left "First Name" showing "e" and "Last Name" empty) — so       problem_user can never satisfy the "Last Name is required" check and can't complete checkout through the UI.
  - **error_user:** filling "Last Name" logs a console TypeError and a failed report to Backtrace, but the typed value still applies and the overview's totals are correct; however, clickin
g "Finish" throws a console TypeError (a minified state-setter reported as "not a function") and the order never completes — the buyer stays on the overview instead of reaching `/checkout-
complete.html`.
  - **visual_user:** completed checkout normally in the checks made (correct totals, correct redirects); no checkout-specific defects found beyond the already-known catalog price/image iss
ues (`specs/product-catalog.md`).
**Starting state.** The cart lives only in localStorage (see `specs/cart.md`), so scenarios start with it filled by the `fillCart` fixture. With products in the cart, a scenario may open `
/checkout-step-one.html` directly, because that's where checkout starts. The buyer is Ada Lovelace, postal code 10001.

**Totals.** The overview's item total, tax and total are checked for carts of every size in `specs/cart.md` 6.1, so they aren't repeated here.

## Test Scenarios

### 1. Completing checkout

**Seed:** `apps/ui/tests/seed.spec.ts`

#### 1.1. Completing checkout empties the cart and returns every product to Add to cart
#### 1.1. The buyer places an order from the cart
                                                                                                                                                                                                **File:** `apps/ui/tests/checkout.spec.ts`

**Steps:**
  1. Starting cart: Sauce Labs Backpack, Sauce Labs Bike Light. Open `/cart.html` and click "Checkout".
    - expect: The URL is `/checkout-step-one.html` and the title reads "Checkout: Your Information".
  2. Fill "First Name" with "Jane", "Last Name" with "Doe" and "Zip/Postal Code" with "12345", then click "Continue".
  2. Fill First Name, Last Name and Zip/Postal Code, then click "Continue".
    - expect: The URL is `/checkout-step-two.html` and the title reads "Checkout: Overview".
  3. Click "Finish".
    - expect: The URL is `/checkout-complete.html` and the title reads "Checkout: Complete!".
    - expect: The page shows an image (alt text "Pony Express"), the heading "Thank you for your order!", the text "Your order has been dispatched, and will arrive just as fast as the pony
 can get there!" and a "Back Home" button.                                                                                                                                                            - expect: The cart badge is gone.
  4. Click "Back Home".
    - expect: The URL is `/inventory.html`.
    - expect: Every one of the 6 products shows "Add to cart", and the cart badge is still gone.
  5. Open `/cart.html`.
    - expect: The title reads "Your Cart", no rows are shown and there's no cart badge.

#### 1.2. The confirmation page confirms the order                                                                                                                                                
**File:** `apps/ui/tests/checkout.spec.ts`
                                                                                                                                                                                                  
**Steps:**                                                                                                                                                                                          1. Starting cart: Sauce Labs Backpack. Submit the information on `/checkout-step-one.html` and click "Finish".
    - expect: The heading reads "Thank you for your order!".
    - expect: The text, the image's alt text and the "Back Home" button match the overview above.

#### 1.3. Placing the order empties the cart

**File:** `apps/ui/tests/checkout.spec.ts`

**Steps:**
  1. Starting cart: Sauce Labs Backpack, Sauce Labs Bike Light. Submit the information.

#### 3.2. Dismissing the error hides it, and the buyer can continue once the missing field is filled
| Information | Error |
|---|---|
| Every field empty | Error: First Name is required |
| First Name empty | Error: First Name is required |
| Last Name empty | Error: Last Name is required |
| Zip/Postal Code empty | Error: Postal Code is required |
| First Name of only spaces | Error: First Name is required |
| Last Name of only spaces | Error: Last Name is required |
| Zip/Postal Code of only spaces | Error: Postal Code is required |

A field of only spaces is as empty as a field left blank.

#### 3.2. Dismissing the error hides it

#### 5.1. Generate PDF order downloads a receipt matching the order

**File:** `apps/ui/tests/checkout.spec.ts`

**Steps:**
  1. Starting cart: Sauce Labs Backpack, Sauce Labs Bike Light. Complete checkout with "First Name" "Jane", "Last Name" "Doe" and "Zip/Postal Code" "12345" through to `/checkout-complete.
html`, then click "Generate PDF order".
    - expect: A PDF downloads, its file name starting with "swag-labs-order-".
    - expect: The PDF's ship-to name and postal code match what was submitted ("Jane Doe", "12345").
    - expect: The PDF lists Sauce Labs Backpack and Sauce Labs Bike Light with their oracle prices, and its Item total, Tax and Total match the cart oracle for this cart (see `specs/cart.
md` 6.1): "$39.98", "$3.20", "$43.18".
- **Finding:** Sauce Labs Onesie's description reads "hemmed **sleeved**" instead of "sleeves" on the overview too, so 2.1 fails on it.
- **Finding:** First Name, Last Name and Zip/Postal Code each accept a value of only spaces and continue to the overview, so the three "only spaces" rows of 3.1 fail.
- **Finding:** every checkout page opens by its URL, whatever state the cart and checkout are in, so 4.1 to 4.6 all fail:
  - Your Information opens with an empty cart and lets the buyer continue.                                                                                                                       - The overview opens with an empty cart ("Item total: $0", without cents, next to "Tax: $0.00" and "Total: $0.00"), and "Finish" then confirms an order of nothing.
  - The overview opens with a filled cart, skipping the information step (also noted in `specs/cart.md`).
  - The confirmation shows "Thank you for your order!" for an order that was never placed, with the products still in the cart.
- Pressing Back on the confirmation page returns to an empty overview whose "Finish" still works. Section 4 covers the same gap.
- "Generate PDF order" appears only when the order was finished in the same page session. The confirmation opened by its URL has only "Back Home".
- The receipt, checked by hand for a Backpack and Bike Light order, shows the buyer's name and postal code, both products with their prices, and "Item total $39.98", "Tax $3.20", "Total $
43.18", all correct. 1.5 checks only the file name: react-pdf compresses the text and stores it as hex glyph codes, so reading it needs a PDF parser, which the repo doesn't have.
- Checkout errors start with "Error:", while the login page's start with "Epic sadface:". The postal code error says "Postal Code" although the field reads "Zip/Postal Code". Neither is c
learly a mistake, so no scenario checks the wording beyond the error texts in 3.1.
- Cancel on the overview goes to the product list, not back to the cart or Your Information. 2.3 treats that as intended.
- During validation, 1.4 failed once in about 350 runs: "Back Home" was clicked but the URL stayed on `/checkout-complete.html`. No cause was found. The confirmation's image loads after t
he page renders and pushes the buttons down 72px, but a delayed image and a layout shift forced mid-click both passed, and 200 more runs of 1.4 passed. The test has no extra wait. If it f
ails again, keep the trace.
- Deviations on other accounts (the scenarios run as standard_user only):
  - **problem_user:** typing into Last Name changes First Name instead, so Last Name stays empty, "Error: Last Name is required" shows and the order can't be placed.
  - **error_user:** Last Name stays empty after filling it, yet "Continue" opens the overview without an error. "Finish" then logs a TypeError and does nothing, so the buyer stays on the
overview with the cart kept.
  - **visual_user** and **performance_glitch_user:** placed an order with the correct totals in the checks made.

❯ Note if you are re using the same user and clear the session it will kill it for all the same users. So maek sure you keep the session open this is alsi very important for the parrallel runs. The test
  that kills the session and reuse the same user must be run last in serial way becase we just simply cant cerate more users

None of the new checkout tests log out or clear cookies—they just reuse the saved standard_user session, though the Logout and Expired session tests in auth.spec.ts do end sessions while running in
parallel. I'll check whether ending a session in one browser context affects other contexts using the same user.

  Ran 2 shell commands
                                                                                                                                                                                                            I confirmed that on SauceDemo, logging out or clearing cookies in one browser context doesn't affect other contexts' sessions, since it's just a client-side cookie with no server-side session—your rule
still guards against apps that do use server sessions. Next, I'll check how to make Playwright run a project last even if earlier tests fail by design.

Playwright supports what we need: teardown projects run after dependent projects regardless of pass/fail, and setting workers: 1 serializes a project's tests. Now I'll list the existing tests that end a

Updated CLAUDE.md (+3 -2)
- Each app config is `defineConfig(baseConfig, { ... })` and adds only what is specific to its service: `use` (base URL, device, headers) and `projects`. Don't repeat a global setting in a
n app config. If every app needs a change, make it in `baseConfig`.
- `defineConfig` merges `use` and `expect` key by key, merges `projects` by name, and lets any other top-level key in the app config replace the base value.
- Projects: each project sets its own `testDir`, `./setup` for setup projects and `./tests` for specs. Leave `testDir` out of the top level of an app config.
  - UI: `precondition` → `auth` → `chromium`. Each runs only if the one before it passed.
  - UI: `precondition` → `auth` → `chromium` → `session-end`. The first three each run only if the one before it passed. `session-end` is the teardown of `auth`, so it runs once `chromium`
 has finished, even if tests failed.
    - `precondition` (`setup/precondition.setup.ts`) checks that the base URL responds. Add any other check that must pass before a login here.
    - `auth` (`setup/auth.setup.ts`) logs in every user in `SESSION_USERS` through the login page and saves each session with `createStorageStateUI` to `apps/ui/.auth/<username>.json`. Tha
t's every user except `locked_out_user`, which SauceDemo refuses. SauceDemo has no login API, so the UI is the only way to get a session.
    - `chromium` runs the specs logged in as `standard_user`. A spec switches user with `test.use({ storageState: storageStatePath(SAUCE_USERS.problem) })`, and tests that start logged out
 use `test.use({ storageState: emptyStorageState })`.
    - SauceDemo keeps the cart only in localStorage. A test that needs products in the cart calls `fillCart([CATALOG.backpack, ...])` before opening a page, instead of clicking Add to cart    . Click through the UI only when adding or removing is what the test checks.
    - A SauceDemo session lasts 10 minutes (the `session-username` cookie), so a saved session only outlives the run that made it by a few minutes.
    - `session-end` runs `tests/session-end.spec.ts` last, one test at a time (`workers: 1`). Every test shares the saved sessions, one per user, and there are no more users to create. A t
est that ends a session (logging out, clearing cookies) could log out every other test using that user, so it goes in `session-end.spec.ts`, never in a `chromium` spec. `chromium` ignores
that file.
  - API: `setup` (`setup/health.setup.ts`) checks that the API responds, then `api` runs the specs.
- Each app writes its own `test-results/` and `playwright-report/` inside its folder. Keep `outputDir` and the reporter output paths explicit in `baseConfig`. Left to Playwright's defaults
, both apps would write to the repo root and overwrite each other.


- `npm run test:ui` or `npm run test:api` runs one app. Its setup projects run first.
- `npm test` runs the API app, then the UI app.
- `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` must pass before a PR.
- `npx playwright test -c apps/ui --project=chromium --no-deps` skips the setup projects and reuses the saved sessions, for local debugging. It only works within 10 minutes of the last fu
ll run.
- `npx playwright test -c apps/ui --project=chromium --no-deps` skips the setup projects and `session-end` and reuses the saved sessions, for local debugging. It only works within 10 minu
tes of the last full run.
- `npx playwright show-report apps/ui/playwright-report` opens an app's last report.

#### 5.2. An expired session sends the user to login on the next page load

**File:** `apps/ui/tests/auth.spec.ts`
**File:** `apps/ui/tests/session-end.spec.ts`

**Steps:**
  1. Starting state: logged in as standard_user, at https://www.saucedemo.com/inventory.html. Remove the `session-username` cookie (e.g. via the browser context's cookie APIs), which is w
hat happens when the 10-minute session expires.


- `performance_glitch_user` takes noticeably longer to log in than the other accounts (roughly 5-6 seconds versus about 1 second). The delay is inside the Login click, which stays within
the configured 10-second action timeout, so no special timeout is needed.
- Both the Username and Password inputs get the red error state and an X-circle icon on every validation failure, even when only one field is the problem. This looks like a minor UI quirk
. It is styling only (CSS classes and icons with no accessible name), so the tests don't assert on it.
- Logout (section 4) and the expired session (5.2) end a session, so they live in `session-end.spec.ts` and run in the `session-end` project, after every other test and one at a time. Eve
ry test shares the saved sessions, one per user. On SauceDemo today, logging out or clearing cookies in one browser context doesn't end another context's session for the same user (checke
d with two contexts sharing one saved session), but a site with server-side sessions would log every test using that user out.
- Direct-URL protected-page errors ignore the query string: opening /inventory-item.html?id=4 while logged out names only '/inventory-item.html'.
- The app does not watch the session cookie: removing `session-username` while a protected page is already shown does not redirect right away. Only the next navigation (a reload or any in
-app link, all of which are full page loads) is blocked.
- Dismissing an error clears the message and the error styling on the inputs, but keeps whatever was typed into Username and Password. A failed login also keeps the typed values.

I'll leave CLAUDE.md as is since it already includes someone else's uncommitted note, and move on to updating the cart plan's note about checkout coverage.
